//! Intersect authored authority and reuse an authored old endpoint when a
//! deliberately relaxed unique key invalidates its former point lookup.
// @ref LLP 3000.000.000#20-visible-rollback-views-roll — no invented row choice or broader audience.
use crate::{
    ast,
    lexer::{self, Kind},
    parser, print,
};
use std::collections::{BTreeMap, BTreeSet};
fn offset(text: &str, line: u32, col: u32) -> usize {
    let start=text.split_inclusive('\n').take(line.saturating_sub(1) as usize).map(str::len).sum::<usize>();
    start+text[start..].chars().take(col.saturating_sub(1) as usize).map(char::len_utf8).sum::<usize>()
}
fn line_span(file: &str, text: &str, site: &crate::Site) -> Option<(usize, usize)> {
    let (tokens, _) = lexer::lex(file, text);
    let at = tokens.iter().position(|t| t.site == *site)?;
    let end = tokens[at..].iter().find(|t| t.kind == Kind::Newline)?;
    Some((
        offset(text, site.line, 1),
        offset(text, end.site.line, end.site.col),
    ))
}
fn declaration(text: &str, line: u32) -> (usize, usize, String) {
    let start = offset(text, line, 1);
    let mut end = text.len();
    for (next, s) in text.lines().enumerate().skip(line as usize) {
        if !s.trim().is_empty() && !s.starts_with(char::is_whitespace) && !s.starts_with("--") {
            end = offset(text, next as u32 + 1, 1);
            break;
        }
    }
    (start, end, text[start..end].to_owned())
}
pub(super) fn carry(
    sources: &mut [(String, String)],
    current: &[(String, String)],
    changes: &mut Vec<String>,
    forced_programs: &BTreeSet<String>,
) -> BTreeSet<String> {
    let docs = current
        .iter()
        .map(|(f, t)| (f, t, parser::parse_document(f, t).0))
        .collect::<Vec<_>>();
    let mut aliases = BTreeMap::new();
    let mut tables = BTreeMap::new();
    let mut programs = BTreeMap::new();
    for (file, text, doc) in &docs {
        for table in &doc.tables {
            tables.insert(table.name.clone(), table);
            if let Some(old) = &table.renamed_from {
                aliases.insert(table.name.clone(), old.clone());
                tables.insert(old.clone(), table);
            }
        }
        for p in &doc.programs {
            programs.insert(super::release_bridge::operation_name(file,&p.name), (*file, *text, p));
        }
        for(name,target,_)in &doc.aliases {if let Some(p)=doc.programs.iter().find(|p|p.name==*target){programs.insert(super::release_bridge::operation_name(file,name),(*file,*text,p));}}
    }
    for(file,text)in sources.iter(){for old in parser::parse_document(file,text).0.tables{if let Some(prior)=&old.renamed_from{if !tables.contains_key(&old.name){if let Some(next)=tables.get(prior).copied(){tables.insert(old.name.clone(),next);aliases.insert(prior.clone(),old.name.clone());}}}}}
    let remap = |expr: &ast::Expr| {
        let mut expr = expr.clone();
        crate::bind::intent_walk::expression(&mut expr, &mut |e| {
            if let ast::Expr::Read(r)
            | ast::Expr::Exists { read: r, .. }
            | ast::Expr::Created { read: r, .. } = e
            {
                if let Some(old) = aliases.get(&r.table) {
                    r.table = old.clone();
                }
            }
        });
        expr
    };
    let mut relaxed = BTreeSet::new();
    let mut expanded = BTreeSet::new();
    for (file, text) in sources {
        let doc = parser::parse_document(file, text).0;
        let mut edits = Vec::new();
        for old in &doc.tables {
            let Some(next) = tables.get(&old.name) else {
                continue;
            };
            let current_text=docs.iter().find(|(_,_,d)|d.tables.iter().any(|t|t.name==next.name)).map(|(_,text,_)|*text).unwrap();
            let authored_read=|rule:&ast::RuleDecl|->Option<String>{
                if rule.effect!=ast::Effect::Read{return None}
                let(a,b)=line_span(&rule.site.file,current_text,&rule.site)?;let raw=&current_text[a..b];
                raw.trim_start().starts_with("read through ").then(||raw.to_owned())
            };
            for index in old.indexes.iter().filter(|i| {
                i.unique
                    && !next
                        .indexes
                        .iter()
                        .any(|n| n.unique && n.columns == i.columns)
            }) {
                if let Some((start, end)) = line_span(file, text, &index.site) {
                    edits.push((start, end, String::new()));
                    relaxed.insert(old.name.clone());
                    changes.push(format!("{}: current source permits multiple rows for {}; current authored endpoint supplies the old-client selection",old.name,index.columns.join(", ")));
                }
            }
            for column in &next.columns {
                if let Some(prior)=old
                    .columns
                    .iter()
                    .find(|c| c.name == column.name || column.renamed_from.as_ref() == Some(&c.name)||c.renamed_from.as_ref()==Some(&column.name))
                {
                    if let Some(edit)=current_default(file,text,prior,column){
                        edits.push(edit);
                        changes.push(format!("{}.{}: retained current authored default",old.name,prior.name));
                    }
                    continue;
                }
                let Some((_, current_text, _)) = docs
                    .iter()
                    .find(|(_, _, d)| d.tables.iter().any(|t| t.name == next.name))
                else {
                    continue;
                };
                if let Some((a, b)) = line_span(&column.site.file, current_text, &column.site) {
                    let at = declaration(text, old.site.line).1;
                    edits.push((at, at, format!("{}\n", &current_text[a..b])));
                    expanded.insert(old.name.clone());
                    changes.push(format!(
                        "{}.{}: retained current guard/input field",
                        old.name, column.name
                    ));
                }
            }
            if next.public.is_none(){if let Some((_,site))=&old.public {if let Some((a,b))=line_span(file,text,site){edits.push((a,b,String::new()));}}}
            if old.public.is_none(){if let Some((_,site))=&next.public {if let Some((a,b))=line_span(&site.file,current_text,site){let at=declaration(text,old.site.line).1;edits.push((at,at,format!("{}\n",&current_text[a..b])));}}}
            for current in &next.rules {
                if old.rules.iter().any(|r| r.effect == current.effect) {
                    continue;
                }
                let at = declaration(text, old.site.line).1;
                if let Some(raw)=authored_read(current){edits.push((at,at,format!("{raw}\n")));continue;}
                let bindings = if current.bindings.is_empty() {
                    String::new()
                } else {
                    format!(" ({})", current.bindings.join(", "))
                };
                edits.push((
                    at,
                    at,
                    format!(
                        "  {}{bindings} <- {}\n",
                        current.effect.word(),
                        print::expression(&remap(&current.value))
                    ),
                ));
                changes.push(format!(
                    "{}: retained current {} authority",
                    old.name,
                    current.effect.word()
                ));
            }
            // Constraints are current authority too: carry authored quotas,
            // immutable fields and final-existence requirements verbatim.
            for (_,site) in &old.immutable {if let Some((a,b))=line_span(file,text,site){edits.push((a,b,String::new()));}}
            for cap in &old.caps {if let Some((a,b))=line_span(file,text,&cap.site){edits.push((a,b,String::new()));}}
            for (_,site) in &old.final_exists {if let Some((a,b))=line_span(file,text,site){edits.push((a,b,String::new()));}}
            let at=declaration(text,old.site.line).1;
            for(fields,_)in &next.immutable {edits.push((at,at,format!("  immutable {}\n",fields.join(", "))));}
            for cap in &next.caps {edits.push((at,at,format!("  cap {} by {}\n",cap.max,cap.by.join(", "))));}
            for(expr,_)in &next.final_exists {edits.push((at,at,format!("  after insert <- {}\n",print::expression(&remap(expr)))));}
            for prior in [&old.expire,&old.not_before].into_iter().flatten(){if let Some((a,b))=line_span(file,text,&prior.1){edits.push((a,b,String::new()));}}
            if let Some((field,_))=&next.expire {edits.push((at,at,format!("  expire at .{field}\n")));}
            if let Some((field,_))=&next.not_before {edits.push((at,at,format!("  not before .{field}\n")));}
            // An authored current endpoint or guard may name a new index. Keep
            // that index with the body; an equal-key old name is not an alias.
            for index in next.indexes.iter().filter(|i|
                !old.indexes.iter().any(|o|o.name==i.name)
                || (i.unique&&!old.indexes.iter().any(|o|o.unique&&o.columns==i.columns))
                || old.indexes.iter().any(|o|o.name==i.name&&o.unique&&!next.indexes.iter().any(|n|n.unique&&n.columns==o.columns))) {
                if let Some((_,current_text,_))=docs.iter().find(|(_,_,d)|d.tables.iter().any(|t|t.name==next.name)) {
                    if let Some((a,b))=line_span(&index.site.file,current_text,&index.site){
                        edits.push((at,at,format!("{}\n",&current_text[a..b])));
                        changes.push(format!("{}: retained current named index {} for authored guards and endpoints",old.name,index.name));
                    }
                }
            }
            let mut replaced = BTreeSet::new();
            for rule in &old.rules {
                let Some((start, end)) = line_span(file, text, &rule.site) else {
                    continue;
                };
                if !replaced.insert((start, end)) {
                    continue;
                }
                let rules = old
                    .rules
                    .iter()
                    .filter(|r| r.site == rule.site)
                    .map(|old_rule| {
                        let effect = old_rule.effect.word();
                        match next.rules.iter().find(|r| r.effect == old_rule.effect) {
                            Some(current) => {
                                if let Some(raw)=authored_read(current){return raw}
                                let bindings = if current.bindings.is_empty() {
                                    String::new()
                                } else {
                                    format!(" ({})", current.bindings.join(", "))
                                };
                                format!(
                                    "  {effect}{bindings} <- {}",
                                    print::expression(&remap(&current.value))
                                )
                            }
                            None if old_rule.effect==ast::Effect::Read&&next.public.is_some()=>String::new(),
                            None => format!("  {effect} <- deny"),
                        }
                    })
                    .collect::<Vec<_>>()
                    .join("\n");
                if text[start..end] != rules {
                    edits.push((start, end, rules));
                    changes.push(format!(
                        "{}: current authored {} authority retained",
                        old.name,
                        rule.effect.word()
                    ));
                }
            }
            for col in &old.columns {
                let Some(next_col) = next
                    .columns
                    .iter()
                    .find(|c| c.name == col.name || c.renamed_from.as_ref() == Some(&col.name))
                else {
                    continue;
                };
                let grouped = next
                    .column_reads
                    .iter()
                    .find(|(fields, _)| fields.contains(&next_col.name))
                    .map(|(_, r)| &r.value);
                let Some(gate) = next_col.read.as_ref().or(grouped) else {
                    continue;
                };
                if col.read.is_none()
                    && !old
                        .column_reads
                        .iter()
                        .any(|(fields, _)| fields.contains(&col.name))
                {
                    let at = declaration(text, old.site.line).1;
                    edits.push((
                        at,
                        at,
                        format!(
                            "  read({}) <- {}\n",
                            col.name,
                            print::expression(&remap(gate))
                        ),
                    ));
                    changes.push(format!(
                        "{}.{}: retained current payload read guard",
                        old.name, col.name
                    ));
                }
            }
        }
        for old in &doc.programs {
            let mut reads = BTreeSet::new();
            let mut d = ast::Document::default();
            d.programs.push(old.clone());
            crate::bind::intent_walk::document(&mut d, &mut |e| match e {
                ast::Expr::Read(r)
                | ast::Expr::Exists { read: r, .. }
                | ast::Expr::Created { read: r, .. } => {
                    reads.insert(r.table.clone());
                }
                ast::Expr::Write { table, .. } => {
                    reads.insert(table.clone());
                }
                _ => (),
            });
            if reads.is_disjoint(&relaxed)
                && reads.is_disjoint(&expanded)
                && !forced_programs.contains(&super::release_bridge::operation_name(file,&old.name))
            {
                continue;
            }
            let Some((current_file, current_text, next)) = programs.get(&super::release_bridge::operation_name(file,&old.name)) else {
                continue;
            };
            if old.kind != next.kind
                || old.args.len() != next.args.len()
                || old
                    .args
                    .iter()
                    .zip(&next.args)
                    .any(|(a, b)| a.name != b.name || !input_accepts(&a.type_, &b.type_))
            {
                continue;
            }
            let (_, _, mut source) = declaration(current_text, next.site.line);
            let (tokens, _) = lexer::lex(current_file, &source);
            let mut replacements = Vec::new();
            if next.name!=old.name {if let Some(name)=tokens.iter().find(|t|t.value==next.name){let at=offset(&source,name.site.line,name.site.col);replacements.push((at,at+name.value.len(),old.name.clone()));}}
            let parsed = parser::parse_document(current_file, &source).0;
            let mut parsed = parsed;
            if let Some(endpoint)=parsed.programs.first() {
                for prior in &old.args {
                    let Some(alias)=&prior.alias else{continue};
                    let Some(arg)=endpoint.args.iter().find(|arg|arg.name==prior.name&&arg.alias.is_none())else{continue};
                    if let Ok((_,end))=super::release_bridge_current::arg_type(&source,&tokens,arg){replacements.push((end,end,format!(" alias {alias}")));}
                }
            }
            crate::bind::intent_walk::document(&mut parsed, &mut |e| {
                let (table, site) = match e {
                    ast::Expr::Read(r)
                    | ast::Expr::Exists { read: r, .. }
                    | ast::Expr::Created { read: r, .. } => (&r.table, &r.site),
                    ast::Expr::Write { table, site, .. } => (&*table, &*site),
                    _ => return,
                };
                if let Some(old) = aliases.get(table) {
                    if let Some(token) = tokens.iter().find(|t| {
                        t.site.line == site.line && t.site.col >= site.col && t.value == *table
                    }) {
                        let at = offset(&source, token.site.line, token.site.col);
                        replacements.push((at, at + table.len(), old.clone()));
                    }
                }
            });
            replacements.sort();
            replacements.dedup();
            replacements.sort_by_key(|r| std::cmp::Reverse(r.0));
            for (a, b, value) in replacements {
                source.replace_range(a..b, &value);
            }
            let (a, b, _) = declaration(text, old.site.line);
            edits.push((a, b, source));
            changes.push(format!("operation {}: current authored compatibility endpoint preserves its argument contract and guards",super::release_bridge::operation_name(file,&old.name)));
        }
        for fill in &doc.backfills {
            let Some(current) = tables.get(&fill.table) else {
                continue;
            };
            if let ast::Expr::Object { fields, .. } = &fill.value {
                if fields
                    .iter()
                    .all(|f| current.columns.iter().any(|c| c.name == f.name))
                {
                    let (a, b, _) = declaration(text, fill.site.line);
                    edits.push((a, b, String::new()));
                    changes.push(format!(
                        "{}: retained current forward migration custody instead of historical {}",
                        fill.table, fill.name
                    ));
                }
            }
        }
        for down in &doc.downfills {
            if docs.iter().any(|(_, _, d)| {
                d.downfills
                    .iter()
                    .any(|f| f.table == down.table && f.name == down.name)
            }) {
                continue;
            }
            let Some(current) = tables.get(&down.table) else {
                continue;
            };
            if let ast::Expr::Object { fields, .. } = &down.value {
                if fields
                    .iter()
                    .all(|f| current.columns.iter().any(|c| c.name == f.name))
                {
                    let (a, b, _) = declaration(text, down.site.line);
                    edits.push((a, b, String::new()));
                    changes.push(format!("{}: obsolete down {} stays historical; visible view keeps current native values",down.table,down.name));
                }
            }
        }
        for (current_file, current_text, current_doc) in &docs {
            for backfill in &current_doc.backfills {
                if !doc.tables.iter().any(|t| t.name == backfill.table) {
                    continue;
                }
                let (_, _, source) = declaration(current_text, backfill.site.line);
                edits.push((text.len(), text.len(), format!("\n{source}")));
                changes.push(format!(
                    "{}: retained current migration {}",
                    backfill.table, backfill.name
                ));
                let _ = current_file;
            }
        }
        edits.sort_by_key(|e| std::cmp::Reverse((e.0, e.1)));
        for (a, b, value) in edits {
            text.replace_range(a..b, &value);
        }
    }
    relaxed.extend(expanded);
    relaxed
}

fn current_default(file:&str,text:&str,old:&ast::ColumnDecl,new:&ast::ColumnDecl)->Option<(usize,usize,String)> {
    let authored=|column:&ast::ColumnDecl|column.default.as_ref().map(print::expression);
    if authored(old)==authored(new){return None;}
    let replacement=authored(new).map(|value|format!("default {value}")).unwrap_or_default();
    if let Some(value)=&old.default {
        let(tokens,_)=lexer::lex(file,text);
        let at=tokens.iter().position(|token|token.site==*value.site())?;
        let keyword=at.checked_sub(1).filter(|i|tokens[*i].value=="default")?;
        let end=at+if matches!(value,ast::Expr::Unary{..}){2}else{1};
        let end=offset(text,tokens.get(end)?.site.line,tokens.get(end)?.site.col).min(text.len());
        Some((offset(text,tokens[keyword].site.line,tokens[keyword].site.col),text[..end].trim_end().len(),replacement))
    }else{
        let(_,end)=super::release_bridge::type_span(file,text,old)?;
        Some((end,end,format!(" {replacement}")))
    }
}

pub(super) fn input_accepts(old: &ast::ScalarType, new: &ast::ScalarType) -> bool {
    if old == new {
        return true;
    }
    if old.optional && !new.optional {
        return false;
    }
    use ast::TypeKind::*;
    let bounds = |k: &ast::TypeKind| match k {
        Int => Some((i64::MIN, i64::MAX)),
        IntRange { min, max } => Some((min.unwrap_or(i64::MIN), max.unwrap_or(i64::MAX))),
        _ => None,
    };
    match (bounds(&old.kind), bounds(&new.kind)) {
        (Some((a, b)), Some((x, y))) => x <= a && y >= b,
        _ => match (&old.kind,&new.kind){(Enum{variants:a},Enum{variants:b})=>a.iter().all(|v|b.contains(v)),_=>old.kind==new.kind},
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use crate::release_bridge::loosen;
    #[test]
    fn preserves_current_transition_guard_fields_and_authored_old_client_writes() {
        let old = "table items:\n  n: int 1..10\n  insert <- allow\n  update <- allow\n  online only\nmutation add(n: int 1..10):\n  return insert items {n}\n";
        let current = "table items:\n  n: int 0..10\n  category: enum('free','paid')\n  insert <- allow\n  update (old,next) <- old.category = next.category\n  online only\nmutation add(n: int 0..10):\n  return insert items {n,category:'free' if n = 0 else 'paid'}\nbackfill items classify(n: int 0..10): {category:'free' if n = 0 else 'paid'}\n";
        let bridge = loosen(
            &[("schema.q".into(), old.into())],
            &[("schema.q".into(), current.into())],
        );
        assert!(
            bridge.diagnostics.is_empty(),
            "{:?}\n{:?}",
            bridge.diagnostics,
            bridge.sources
        );
        let text = &bridge.sources[0].1;
        assert!(text.contains("old.category = next.category"));
        assert!(text.contains("category:'free' if n = 0 else 'paid'"));
        assert!(text.contains("backfill items classify"));
    }
    #[test]
    fn keeps_a_fitting_old_write_when_current_guards_transport_without_replacing_it(){
        let old="table items:\n  amount: int\n  insert <- allow\n  online only\nmutation add():\n  return insert items {amount:100}\n";
        let current=old.replace("amount: int", "amount: int ?").replace("amount:100", "amount:null");
        let b=loosen(&[("schema.q".into(),old.into())],&[("schema.q".into(),current)]);assert!(b.diagnostics.is_empty(),"{:?}",b.diagnostics);assert!(b.sources[0].1.contains("amount:100"));
    }
    #[test]
    fn carries_current_guards_even_when_the_join_has_no_changed_columns(){
        let old="table items:\n  n: int\n  insert <- allow\n  online only\nmutation add(n: int):\n  return insert items {n}\n";
        let current=old.replace("  return insert items","  require n > 0 else TOO_EARLY\n  return insert items");
        let b=loosen(&[("schema.q".into(),old.into())],&[("schema.q".into(),current)]);assert!(b.diagnostics.is_empty(),"{:?}",b.diagnostics);assert!(b.sources[0].1.contains("TOO_EARLY"));
    }
    #[test]
    fn does_not_reuse_an_endpoint_that_requires_new_arguments() {
        let old = "table items:\n  n: int\n  insert <- allow\n  online only\nmutation add(n: int):\n  return insert items {n}\n";
        let current = "table items:\n  n: int\n  required: int\n  insert <- allow\n  online only\nmutation add(n: int, required: int):\n  return insert items {n,required}\n";
        let bridge = loosen(
            &[("schema.q".into(), old.into())],
            &[("schema.q".into(), current.into())],
        );
        assert!(!bridge.diagnostics.is_empty());
    }
}
//! Carry authored guards; never invent a value for a newly optional field.
// @ref LLP 3000.000.000#20-visible-rollback-views-roll — compilation gates automatic registration.
use crate::{ast::Stmt, parser, print};
use std::collections::BTreeSet;

// Guard meaning depends on statement order and named definitions, not merely
// the last textual binding. Compare declared row identities, then select
// current execution when that guarded context cannot be transported intact.
// Field/table authority is validated separately before choosing a strategy.
pub(super) fn current_context_needed(
    sources:&[(String,String)],current:&[(String,String)],
    view_schema:&snapback4_core::Schema,current_schema:&snapback4_core::Schema,
)->bool {
    let view_docs=sources.iter().map(|(f,t)|parser::parse_document(f,t).0).collect::<Vec<_>>();
    let mut next_docs=current.iter().map(|(f,t)|parser::parse_document(f,t).0).collect::<Vec<_>>();
    let mut names=current_schema.table_renames.iter().filter(|(_,old)|view_schema.tables.contains_key(*old)).map(|(new,old)|(new.clone(),old.clone())).collect::<std::collections::BTreeMap<_,_>>();
    for(name,prior)in &view_schema.table_renames {
        if current_schema.tables.contains_key(prior)&&!current_schema.tables.contains_key(name){names.insert(prior.clone(),name.clone());}
    }
    for doc in &mut next_docs{normalize_identities(doc,&names);}
    if super::release_bridge_indexes::current_context_needed(&view_docs,&next_docs,view_schema,current_schema,&names){return true;}
    let exports=|docs:&[crate::ast::Document]| {
        let mut out=std::collections::BTreeMap::new();
        for doc in docs {
            for p in &doc.programs{out.insert(super::release_bridge::operation_name(&doc.file,&p.name),p.clone());}
            for(name,target,_)in &doc.aliases{if let Some(p)=doc.programs.iter().find(|p|p.name==*target){out.insert(super::release_bridge::operation_name(&doc.file,name),p.clone());}}
        }out
    };
    let shapes=|docs:&[crate::ast::Document]| {
        let mut out=std::collections::BTreeMap::new();
        for doc in docs {
            for shape in &doc.shapes {
                let fields=shape.fields.iter().map(|field|match field {
                    crate::ast::ShapeField::Spread{..}=>"...".into(),
                    crate::ast::ShapeField::NamedSpread{name,..}=>format!("...{name}"),
                    crate::ast::ShapeField::Bare{name,..}=>name.clone(),
                    crate::ast::ShapeField::Field{name,value,..}=>format!("{name}:{}",expression_signature(value)),
                }).collect::<Vec<_>>();
                out.insert(shape.name.clone(),fields);
            }
        }out
    };
    let shapes_changed=shapes(&view_docs)!=shapes(&next_docs);
    let view=exports(&view_docs);let next=exports(&next_docs);
    for(name,p)in next {
        let Some(old)=view.get(&name)else{continue};
        let mut sensitive=p.prelude.iter().chain(&p.body).any(|s|matches!(s,Stmt::Require{..}|Stmt::If{..}|Stmt::For{..}|Stmt::As{..}));
        let mut doc=crate::ast::Document::default();doc.programs.push(p.clone());
        crate::bind::intent_walk::document(&mut doc,&mut |expr|{
            sensitive|=matches!(expr,crate::ast::Expr::Read(_)|crate::ast::Expr::Exists{..}|crate::ast::Expr::Created{..}|crate::ast::Expr::Walk{..}|crate::ast::Expr::Merge(_)|crate::ast::Expr::If{..}|crate::ast::Expr::Assert{..}|crate::ast::Expr::Call{..}|crate::ast::Expr::Write{condition:Some(_),..});
        });
        let body=|p:&crate::ast::ProgramDecl|p.prelude.iter().chain(&p.body).map(normalized_statement).collect::<Vec<_>>();
        if sensitive&&(body(old)!=body(&p)||shapes_changed){return true;}
    }
    false
}

// Only typed row-reference slots change spelling. Literal values, member names,
// helper identities and business strings retain their authored meaning.
fn normalize_identities(doc:&mut crate::ast::Document,names:&std::collections::BTreeMap<String,String>) {
    fn kind(ty:&mut crate::ast::ScalarType,names:&std::collections::BTreeMap<String,String>){
        match &mut ty.kind {
            crate::ast::TypeKind::Ref{table}=>if let Some(old)=names.get(table){*table=old.clone();},
            crate::ast::TypeKind::List{item,..}=>kind(item,names),
            _=>(),
        }
    }
    for p in &mut doc.programs{for a in &mut p.args{kind(&mut a.type_,names);}}
    for f in &mut doc.functions{for a in &mut f.args{kind(&mut a.type_,names);}}
    let table=|name:&mut String|{if let Some(old)=names.get(name){*name=old.clone();}};
    crate::bind::intent_walk::document(doc,&mut |expr|match expr {
        crate::ast::Expr::Read(r)|crate::ast::Expr::Exists{read:r,..}|crate::ast::Expr::Created{read:r,..}=>table(&mut r.table),
        crate::ast::Expr::Write{table:name,..}|crate::ast::Expr::Walk{table:name,..}=>table(name),
        crate::ast::Expr::Merge(m)=>{table(&mut m.table);for lane in &mut m.lanes{if let Some(name)=&mut lane.table{table(name);}if let Some(r)=&mut lane.planned{table(&mut r.table);}if let Some((_,r))=&mut lane.nulls{table(&mut r.table);}}},
        _=>(),
    });
}

pub(super) fn carry(
    sources: &mut [(String, String)],
    current: &[(String, String)],
    _affected: &BTreeSet<String>,
    changes: &mut Vec<String>,
) -> (Vec<crate::Diagnostic>,BTreeSet<String>) {
    let mut diagnostics = Vec::new();
    let mut fallback=BTreeSet::new();
    let mut current_documents = current
        .iter()
        .map(|(file, text)| parser::parse_document(file, text).0)
        .collect::<Vec<_>>();
    let target_documents = sources
        .iter()
        .map(|(file, text)| parser::parse_document(file, text).0)
        .collect::<Vec<_>>();
    let target_tables=target_documents.iter().flat_map(|d|&d.tables).map(|t|t.name.clone()).collect::<BTreeSet<_>>();
    let current_tables=current_documents.iter().flat_map(|d|&d.tables).map(|t|t.name.clone()).collect::<BTreeSet<_>>();
    let mut identities=std::collections::BTreeMap::new();
    for table in current_documents.iter().flat_map(|d|&d.tables) {
        if let Some(prior)=table.renamed_from.as_ref().filter(|prior|target_tables.contains(*prior)){identities.insert(table.name.clone(),prior.clone());}
    }
    for table in target_documents.iter().flat_map(|d|&d.tables) {
        if let Some(prior)=table.renamed_from.as_ref().filter(|prior|current_tables.contains(*prior)&&!current_tables.contains(&table.name)){identities.insert(prior.clone(),table.name.clone());}
    }
    for doc in &mut current_documents{normalize_identities(doc,&identities);}
    let helper_signatures = |docs: &[crate::ast::Document]| {
        let mut out=std::collections::BTreeMap::new();
        for doc in docs {
            for f in &doc.functions {
                let args=f.args.iter().map(|a|(a.name.clone(),a.type_.clone(),a.alias.clone(),a.default.as_ref().map(expression_signature),a.deprecated)).collect::<Vec<_>>();
                let body=match &f.body {
                    crate::ast::FunctionBody::Expression(e)=>vec![format!("expression {}",expression_signature(e))],
                    crate::ast::FunctionBody::Block(body)=>body.iter().map(normalized_statement).collect(),
                };
                let mut function=crate::ast::Document::default();function.functions.push(f.clone());
                let mut calls=Vec::new();crate::bind::intent_walk::document(&mut function,&mut |e|if let crate::ast::Expr::Call{name,..}=e{calls.push(name.clone());});
                out.insert(f.name.clone(),((args,body),calls));
            }
        }out
    };
    let current_helpers = helper_signatures(&current_documents);
    let target_helpers = helper_signatures(&target_documents);
    let mut next=std::collections::BTreeMap::new();
    for doc in &current_documents {
        for p in &doc.programs {next.insert(super::release_bridge::operation_name(&doc.file,&p.name),p.clone());}
        for(name,target,_)in &doc.aliases {if let Some(p)=doc.programs.iter().find(|p|p.name==*target){let mut exported=p.clone();exported.name=name.clone();next.insert(super::release_bridge::operation_name(&doc.file,name),exported);}}
    }
    for (file, text) in sources {
        let doc = parser::parse_document(file, text).0;
        let mut edits = Vec::new();
        for old in doc.programs {
            let Some(new) = next.get(&super::release_bridge::operation_name(file,&old.name)) else {
                continue;
            };
            // The whole selected source must retain current matching guards,
            // including changes unrelated to the column that triggered a view.
            let guards = old
                .prelude
                .iter()
                .chain(&old.body)
                .filter_map(guard)
                .collect::<BTreeSet<_>>();
            let added = new
                .prelude
                .iter()
                .chain(&new.body)
                .filter_map(guard)
                .filter(|g| !guards.contains(g))
                .collect::<BTreeSet<_>>();
            let bindings = |p: &crate::ast::ProgramDecl| {
                p.prelude
                    .iter()
                    .chain(&p.body)
                    .filter_map(|s| {
                        if let Stmt::Bind { names, value, .. } = s {
                            Some((names, value))
                        } else {
                            None
                        }
                    })
                    .flat_map(|(names, value)| {
                        names.iter().enumerate().map(move |(at, name)| {
                            (
                                name.clone(),
                                (format!("{at}:{}", expression_signature(value)), value.clone()),
                            )
                        })
                    })
                    .collect::<std::collections::BTreeMap<_, _>>()
            };
            let old_bindings = bindings(&old);
            let new_bindings = bindings(new);
            let mut dependent = BTreeSet::new();
            let mut helpers = BTreeSet::new();
            let collect = |expr: &crate::ast::Expr,
                           names: &mut BTreeSet<String>,
                           helpers: &mut BTreeSet<String>| {
                let mut expr = expr.clone();
                crate::bind::intent_walk::expression(&mut expr, &mut |e| match e {
                    crate::ast::Expr::Name { name, .. } => {
                        names.insert(name.clone());
                    }
                    crate::ast::Expr::Call { name, .. } => {
                        helpers.insert(name.clone());
                    }
                    _ => (),
                });
            };
            for s in new.prelude.iter().chain(&new.body) {
                if let Stmt::Require { condition, .. } = s {
                    collect(condition, &mut dependent, &mut helpers);
                }
            }
            let mut visited = BTreeSet::new();
            let mut unsafe_binding = false;
            while let Some(name) = dependent.pop_first() {
                if !visited.insert(name.clone()) {
                    continue;
                }
                if let Some((signature, value)) = new_bindings.get(&name) {
                    if old_bindings.get(&name).map(|(s, _)| s) != Some(signature)
                        || has_write(value)
                    {
                        unsafe_binding = true;
                    }
                    collect(value, &mut dependent, &mut helpers);
                } else if old_bindings.contains_key(&name) {
                    unsafe_binding = true;
                }
            }
            // Reused authored workflows retain their complete helper meaning,
            // including helpers feeding writes, returns and system scopes.
            let mut workflow = crate::ast::Document::default();
            workflow.programs.push(new.clone());
            crate::bind::intent_walk::document(&mut workflow, &mut |e| {
                if let crate::ast::Expr::Call { name, .. } = e {
                    helpers.insert(name.clone());
                }
            });
            let mut visited = BTreeSet::new();
            let mut unsafe_helper = false;
            while let Some(name) = helpers.pop_first() {
                // Helper declarations are global after the compiler validates
                // optional file qualification. Compare the resolved identity.
                let name=name.rsplit('.').next().unwrap_or(&name).to_owned();
                if !visited.insert(name.clone()) {
                    continue;
                }
                if current_helpers.get(&name).map(|s| &s.0)
                    != target_helpers.get(&name).map(|s| &s.0)
                {
                    unsafe_helper = true;
                }
                if let Some((_, calls)) = current_helpers.get(&name) {
                    helpers.extend(calls.iter().cloned());
                }
            }
            let nested = |p: &crate::ast::ProgramDecl| {
                p.body
                    .iter()
                    .filter(|s| matches!(s, Stmt::If { .. } | Stmt::For { .. } | Stmt::As { .. }))
                    .map(|s| normalized_statement(s))
                    .collect::<Vec<_>>()
            };
            if unsafe_binding || unsafe_helper || nested(&old) != nested(new) {
                fallback.insert(super::release_bridge::operation_name(file,&old.name));
                diagnostics.push(crate::diag::type_error(&old.site,format!("rollback guard dependency closure for {} differs; an old binding/helper or control-flow path cannot establish the current guard",old.name),"retain a compatible guarded old workflow or declare intentional hiding of the unrepresentable values").guide("deploy"));
                continue;
            }
            if added.is_empty() {
                continue;
            }
            // Keep the old workflow; add current requirements after its reads,
            // immediately before the first effect/return. Undefined or changed
            // bindings fail ordinary compilation rather than guessing policy.
            let at = old
                .body
                .iter()
                .find(|s| {
                    !matches!(s, Stmt::Require { .. })
                        && !matches!(s,Stmt::Bind{value,..} if !has_write(value))
                })
                .map(stmt_site)
                .unwrap_or(old.site.line + 1);
            let offset = text
                .split_inclusive('\n')
                .take(at.saturating_sub(1) as usize)
                .map(str::len)
                .sum::<usize>();
            edits.push((
                offset,
                added.iter().map(|g| format!("  {g}\n")).collect::<String>(),
            ));
            for g in added {
                changes.push(format!("operation {}: carried current {g}", old.name));
            }
        }
        edits.sort_by_key(|(at, _)| std::cmp::Reverse(*at));
        for (at, guard) in edits {
            text.insert_str(at, &guard);
        }
    }
    (diagnostics,fallback)
}
fn guard(s: &Stmt) -> Option<String> {
    match s {
        Stmt::Require {
            condition,
            code,
            message,
            ..
        } => Some(format!(
            "require {} else {}{}",
            print::expression(condition),
            code,
            message
                .as_ref()
                .map(|m| format!(" '{}'", m.replace('\\', "\\\\").replace('\'', "\\'")))
                .unwrap_or_default()
        )),
        _ => None,
    }
}
fn stmt_site(s: &Stmt) -> u32 {
    match s {
        Stmt::As { site, .. }
        | Stmt::Bind { site, .. }
        | Stmt::Expr { site, .. }
        | Stmt::Require { site, .. }
        | Stmt::Return { site, .. }
        | Stmt::Emit { site, .. }
        | Stmt::If { site, .. }
        | Stmt::For { site, .. }
        | Stmt::Break { site }
        | Stmt::Continue { site } => site.line,
    }
}

fn has_write(e: &crate::ast::Expr) -> bool {
    let mut e = e.clone();
    let mut writes = false;
    crate::bind::intent_walk::expression(&mut e, &mut |e| {
        if matches!(e, crate::ast::Expr::Write { .. }) {
            writes = true;
        }
    });
    writes
}

// A source printer is deliberately lossy (for example it prints a merge's
// header without its lanes). Security comparison retains every AST field and
// strips only diagnostic locations. Exhaustive Expr matching makes new
// expression forms require an explicit location-normalization decision.
fn expression_signature(value:&crate::ast::Expr)->String {
    use crate::ast::{Expr,ShapeField};
    fn shape(fields:&mut [ShapeField]) {
        for field in fields {
            match field {
                ShapeField::Spread{site}|ShapeField::NamedSpread{site,..}|ShapeField::Bare{site,..}|ShapeField::Field{site,..}=>*site=crate::Site::default(),
            }
        }
    }
    fn read(r:&mut crate::ast::ReadExpr) {
        r.site=crate::Site::default();
        for k in &mut r.keys{k.site=crate::Site::default();}
        for seek in &mut r.seeks{seek.site=crate::Site::default();}
        if let Some(range)=&mut r.range{range.site=crate::Site::default();}
    }
    let mut value=value.clone();
    crate::bind::intent_walk::expression(&mut value,&mut |expr| {
        match expr {
            Expr::Read(r)=>read(r),
            Expr::Exists{read:r,site}|Expr::Created{read:r,site}=>{read(r);*site=crate::Site::default();},
            Expr::Merge(m)=>{
                m.site=crate::Site::default();
                for lane in &mut m.lanes {
                    lane.site=crate::Site::default();
                    if let Some(fields)=&mut lane.project{shape(fields);}
                    if let Some(r)=&mut lane.planned{read(r);}
                    if let Some((_,r))=&mut lane.nulls{read(r);}
                }
            },
            Expr::Object{fields,site}=>{*site=crate::Site::default();for f in fields{f.site=crate::Site::default();}},
            Expr::Shape{fields,site,..}=>{*site=crate::Site::default();shape(fields);},
            Expr::Write{keys,fields,site,..}=>{*site=crate::Site::default();for k in keys{k.site=crate::Site::default();}for f in fields{f.site=crate::Site::default();}},
            Expr::Name{site,..}|Expr::Focal{site}|Expr::Literal{site,..}|Expr::Array{site,..}
            |Expr::Unary{site,..}|Expr::Binary{site,..}|Expr::Member{site,..}|Expr::Call{site,..}
            |Expr::Assert{site,..}|Expr::ShapeUse{site,..}|Expr::Walk{site,..}|Expr::If{site,..}=>*site=crate::Site::default(),
        }
    });
    format!("{value:?}")
}

fn normalized_statement(s: &Stmt) -> String {
    match s {
        Stmt::As {
            principal, body, ..
        } => format!(
            "as {} {:?}",
            expression_signature(principal),
            body.iter().map(normalized_statement).collect::<Vec<_>>()
        ),
        Stmt::Bind { names, value, .. } => format!("bind {names:?} {}", expression_signature(value)),
        Stmt::Expr { value, .. } => format!("expr {}", expression_signature(value)),
        Stmt::Require {
            condition,
            code,
            message,
            ..
        } => format!(
            "require {} {code:?} {message:?}",
            expression_signature(condition)
        ),
        Stmt::Return { value, .. } => format!("return {}", expression_signature(value)),
        Stmt::Emit {
            effect,
            payload,
            once,
            ..
        } => format!(
            "emit {effect} {} {:?}",
            expression_signature(payload),
            once.as_ref().map(expression_signature)
        ),
        Stmt::If {
            condition,
            then_body,
            else_body,
            ..
        } => format!(
            "if {} {:?} {:?}",
            expression_signature(condition),
            then_body
                .iter()
                .map(normalized_statement)
                .collect::<Vec<_>>(),
            else_body
                .iter()
                .map(normalized_statement)
                .collect::<Vec<_>>()
        ),
        Stmt::For {
            name, domain, body, ..
        } => format!(
            "for {name} {} {:?}",
            expression_signature(domain),
            body.iter().map(normalized_statement).collect::<Vec<_>>()
        ),
        Stmt::Break { .. } => "break".into(),
        Stmt::Continue { .. } => "continue".into(),
    }
}

## Summary

Snapback 4 is one of two parallel candidates for the successor to Snapback 2,
and the sibling of Snapback 3, not its successor. It builds
[LLP 2000.000](./2000.000-keep-the-core-flip-two-substrates.rfc.md) as
written: the Snapback 2 core kept verbatim, two substrates flipped up front —
resources metered at runtime instead of priced statically, and a client that
replicates the viewer's declared partition instead of caching receipted
results — proved first on the hub fixture, with a deterministic simulator as
the primary harness. Snapback 3 ([LLP 2000](./2000-snapback3.rfc.md), led by
Codex / GPT-6 Astra) keeps the same two questions open and discovers their
answers in its first slice. Snapback 2 ([LLP 1000](./1000-snapback2.explainer.md))
continues as the incumbent and the control. All three are judged by one
pre-registered comparison on one clock.

This document records the author's decisions of 2026-09-12 that created the
track, restates the bet on one page so a reader can orient without the brief,
fixes the bakeoff protocol, draws the fence, and plans the first four weeks.
The design argument and its evidence stay in LLP 2000.000. Only running code
will bind (`rules/RULES.md`).

## 1. Decisions (author, 2026-09-12)

On 2026-09-12 Charlie Cheever asked whether Claude Fable 5.1 should lead a
Snapback 4 while Codex / GPT-6 Astra leads Snapback 3 and Snapback 2
continues, "and see which one turns out best." The lead answered yes with
conditions; the author accepted them ("Sounds good") and asked for this
document. The decisions below are therefore the lead's proposal as accepted
by the author. Where the author has not separately ruled, the text says so.

1. **Three tracks, one comparison.** Snapback 2 continues as the incumbent
   and the control. Snapback 3 builds LLP 2000; Snapback 4 builds LLP
   2000.000. This is a bakeoff of *bets* — discover the substrates in the
   slice versus decide them first — not of models. The result will say more
   about the bets than about the leads, and §3 is written so that it can.
2. **The name is Snapback 4.** Siblings, not a sequence: "4" does not mean
   "after 3." A separate number gives the track its own corpus root,
   directory, package identity and data directory with no rename when the
   bakeoff resolves, and files never move in LLP. If Snapback 4 loses, it is
   a number that never shipped. "Snapback 3b" was considered and rejected:
   it reads as a variant of 3, and this is a different bet.
3. **Snapback 4 builds LLP 2000.000 as written.** Its §14 open questions are
   the lead's to decide inside the track, recorded in §7 as they are
   decided. The author rules on the bar, the comparison and promotion (§3).
4. **One owner on the core.** The lead writes the engine, the store and the
   client contract. Codex lanes build the fixture generator, the reference
   applications, the device witnesses and the periphery. Blind reviews from
   two model families are reserved for the two disclosure-sensitive
   mechanisms: partition inversion of read rules (LLP 2000.000 §7.1) and
   the meter's cut behaviour on non-uniform scans (LLP 2000.000 §6). This
   departs from the standing rule that Codex codes and Claude orchestrates,
   deliberately, for the core only.
5. **The clock is four weeks** from the first commit under `snapback4/` to
   the first slice running on the hub fixture. At the clock the author kills
   the track, merges its substrates into a survivor, or continues it. There
   is no third month without a ruling.
6. **Shared and not shared.** Shared with Snapback 3: the LLP corpus, the
   Clique harness and scorers, the fixtures, the briefs, and the simulator
   once either track has one. Not shared: code, except the Snapback 2 pieces
   both are expected to copy (LLP 2000.000 §5.1).
7. **Snapback 2 keeps shipping and freezes new substrate work for the
   duration.** Fix-forward, the loop bar, acquisition and papercuts continue;
   no new certificate axes, client custody mechanisms or ceiling changes.
   This is accepted here as a condition of the bakeoff. Making it binding on
   concurrent Snapback 2 sessions needs one line in `rules/NOT-DOING.md`'s
   Moves ledger, which this document does not write.
8. **Reading load.** This root, updated in place, and one append-only
   journal, `snapback4/JOURNAL.md`. Sub-LLPs only for a decision that must
   outlive the journal. No panels beyond decision 4's two reviews.

## 2. The bet on one page

**Keep verbatim from Snapback 2** (LLP 2000.000 §5.1): the rule language
with its uniformity and hoist analysis, `decided_true` and the
structural-fact refusals; one data language, Quarry, with `--` comments,
`require … else CODE`, `shape`, `merge`, `walk`, `chunk`; the value model
and its conformance corpus; typed read and write states with no silent state
and the refusal envelope `{code, family, site, message, rewrite, guide}`; the
successor classifier (widening additive, checked narrowing, exact projection
ownership); `deliver`'s engine-computed audience, tombstones and
`bootstrap`; `schedule`, `job`, `chunk` and rehearsal; personas; `init`,
`dev`, `check`, `doctor`, `run`, `test`, `why`, `trace`, `guide`; H0
`deploy`, `rollback`, `secret`; channels; assets; the word-prefix index.

**Flip 1 — certify authorization, meter resources** (LLP 2000.000 §6). The
certificate keeps every read site's shape, uniformity, completeness class
and prediction eligibility, and stops pricing bytes and steps. A
deterministic meter charges examined rows, index entries, rule probes,
steps, heap, output and rows written as the program runs, on the server and
in the client interpreter alike. A query cut by the meter returns `capped`
with no continuation past the cut, the same observable outcome as a static
ceiling cut today; a mutation aborts with `E_BOUND` naming actual work; a
job is chunked. `check --cost` prints the structural cost class; `test`
prints measured meters on the hub fixture.

**Flip 2 — the viewer's partition is the unit of sync** (LLP 2000.000 §7).
`sync to <audience> last N by <index>` on a table makes the engine maintain,
per principal, the rows that principal may read, as a change log with a
horizon; a table without the word is online-only, and `check` says which
rule term prevented inversion. The client holds a replica of its partitions,
one watermark each, a durable outbox with client-minted ids, and the
interpreter; queries run locally and are `complete` inside the horizon.
Revocation is a scope tombstone; `until-revoked` and `delivered-history`
survive as the client's leave policy. Two client tiers — online-only
without a store, local-first with one — share one language and one server.

**The vocabulary is fixed before the mechanism** (LLP 2000.000 §8):

```ts
type Read<T> = { data: T; complete: boolean; fresh: boolean; since?: number; next?: Cursor }
            | { loading: true } | { denied: Refusal };
row.pending: boolean;
type Write = { state: "pending" } | { state: "sent"; seq: number } | { state: "failed"; why: Refusal };
type Link = "online" | "offline" | "signed-out";
```

A screen that needs a word not on that card is a defect in the mechanism,
not a gap in the card.

**The hub comes first** (LLP 2000.000 §11): the Clique generator's `u0001`
with 1,000 memberships, `u0500` with 500 follows, and about 100,000
messages are the first fixture; day-two changes are designed before the
day-one screen; the native client is in slice one; physical floors are
measured before targets; the simulator is the primary harness.

the same three scorers as the 2026-09-05 run (gpt-6-astra, grok-4.6,

### 19.5 Argument aliases and review evidence

`explanation: text 1..500 alias reason` declares one canonical argument and
one accepted old spelling on a Quarry query/mutation. Normalize the old
spelling to the canonical value before typed execution, including local
device execution. Both spellings supplied at once refuse E_INPUT even if
equal. Alias names must be unique and cannot collide with canonical args;
deprecated/aliased combinations refuse. Canonical default/null validation
semantics are unchanged. Generated TypeScript accepts either spelling and
rejects both; capability comparison recognizes the old shape accepted by
the alias. Compatibility advice for renamed same-typed inputs recommends
mapping with an alias. It never recommends accepting and dropping the old
value with `deprecated` as a rename repair.

Every changed guide fence/starter runs as written against a freshly packed
tarball. Qualification includes regression stores, actual old callers,
reverse/native/preimage conflicts, forward old-source writes, inspection,
hot/cold/check parity and copied wave-19 dormant lanes. Full test and
differential results and any external qualification limits belong in
WORKSTREAM-REPORT.md. Reviews are mutually blind Muse and fresh Codex
sessions on one immutable semantic revision, retained verbatim with
provenance. A READY verdict records review readiness, not author acceptance
or a publication receipt. No version bump or push is authorized.

### 19.6 Review record

Six mutually blind design rounds are preserved under `llp/reviews/3000.000.000-dorm-r*`. Round 6 received READY from Muse `muse-spark-1.3-contributor` (high, wrapper fallback) and a fresh Codex `gpt-6.1-sol` (high) on the same immutable snapshot; provenance includes its SHA-256 and both exact response digests. Earlier NOT READY findings drove disjoint writer admission, closed scalar/lifetime boundaries, per-cell restoration, explicit null/presence semantics and repeated-cycle recomputation. These are design readiness verdicts, not an implementation test result, author acceptance or a publication receipt. The commissioned implementation and qualification results are recorded in the workstream report.

A final narrowly scoped confirmation (r7) also returned READY from both providers: protected rename kinds reject during compilation, preventing a forward-only protected transition. The unchanged full amendment remains covered by the r6 pair. Both exact outputs and delta provenance are preserved.

## 20. Visible rollback views (ROLL)

Implementer: Codex for Charlie Cheever, 2026-10-22; commissioned for 0.4.39.
This amendment replaces opt-in bridging for declared domain widenings and
relaxed unique keys. Existing retention, metering, authority, immutable source,
and atomic activation laws continue to apply. SEC7 owns ordinary check gates
and ranking; ROLL owns deploy presentation and release selection.

### 20.1 Prepared source and selection

Deploy compares every selectable ordinary predecessor, even without sampled
widened rows. A widening without an applicable authored down, or a foresight
finding that predecessor-table rows would be hidden or misrepresented, requires
a compiled rollback view. Newer-only tables remain an informational distinction;
their absence from an old release alone does not require a view. A typed down
covering a removed text column also covers that column's old text constraint;
the activation transaction still validates its actual projected values. All needed views must prepare successfully or deploy refuses.
View admission compares structural source domains (including text minima and
formats), nominal aliases, unique keys and caps; it never parses human advice
strings to decide whether a view is needed. A fitting authored scalar down
removes its covered value-domain risk; extraction/renames retain their typed
identity laws.
The compatible join is LLP 3000.000.000.002 §8: optional union, integer and text
bound union with identical format, enum union, recursively joined bounded lists,
and asset size-bound union with identical nominal kind, MIME and once policy.
Nullability and list cardinality join separately. This differs from extraction,
which requires full type equality, including size, optionality and cardinality.
References join only across explicit nominal identity renames. A mixed change
cannot enlarge any current shared-column domain with predecessor-only values:
current read/write predicates may rely on their schema-level impossibility.
The ordinary union view refuses even if that column is not the widening trigger.
A closed alternative may retain the complete current authored source closure
(schema, defaults, programs, helpers, jobs and authority), rather than synthesize
a union. It requires every predecessor table and column under the same spelling,
every predecessor operation name/kind, and current argument domains accepting
every predecessor input under the same closed compatibility rule below. It
retains the exact current domains and values, with no resurrection, translation
or old-only writes. Missing fields/names or incompatible inputs make this
complete-closure strategy unavailable; preparation then tries the selective
guarded bridge below. Current
result semantics are disclosed for old-UI review; current extra operations
remain current-authorized. Native/worker bundle qualification remains mandatory.
Otherwise author a compatible source and truthful migration before deploying. Enum order
and nominal rename spelling do not change this domain comparison. Domain
includes nullability, recursive list-element/cardinality bounds, text formats,
integer bounds, asset kind/size/MIME/once constraints; unsupported or incompatible
domain changes refuse rather than truncate or coerce. No conversion,
zero-money fallback, implicit business default or arbitrary row choice is inferred.

The strict strategy retains predecessor operation names and argument contracts;
the guarded-current strategy below may explicitly pause removed writes. Both
retain the required read surface and contracts of retained exports, including
existing authored operation aliases under their compilation law (same-module,
no alias chains). Every exported predecessor alias counts as an old name; a
current alias resolves to its actual authored target for input, guard, helper
and authority comparison. Renaming an export never drops its current guards. A current
require can be copied only with identical argument and entire transitive local
binding/helper meanings, lookup predicates and control-flow paths. A changed
unmodeled closure or guard depending on writes refuses. Canonical expression
comparison ignores source locations, never semantics. When a relaxed unique key invalidates an old lookup, or current guards require
an added field on a shared table, retain that field and its authored current
backfill. An affected operation whose current guard/workflow cannot be transported
unchanged may likewise reuse its current authored endpoint under the same closed
input-compatibility rule. Every current added field is retained on a shared table, even without a guard
mention; required fields retain their authored default/backfill and every old
write path must supply them through a compatible current authored endpoint or
refuse compilation. No missing-required-field write is permitted. All helpers in a reused workflow, including write,
return and system-scope dependencies, need identical transitive meanings. An already-authored current endpoint of the same name/kind whose
arguments accept every predecessor input may replace the affected old endpoint;
the initial comparison permits identical argument types, optional widening and
integer-bound widening and enum-superset widening, never narrowing or new required arguments. Retained
current backfill identity keeps its output custody active. This supplies authored
writes for the added required fields instead of inventing defaults; the generator never invents first-row
selection. The replacement's point/limited selection must retain the language's
deterministic total ordering, including its stable id tie-break; a source outside
that deterministic language fragment refuses. Missing or incompatible authored
endpoints refuse compilation.

Authored current named indexes travel with any current endpoint, helper or guard
that uses them. An equivalent historical key under another name is not an alias.
Adding such an index does not invent a selection or relax an authority constraint.
A shared name also does not prove the same interpretation: compare its complete
compiled ordered components, including token/calendar/contribution contracts,
after injective declared table and plain-column identity alignment. Changed
meaning requires the complete current execution context under the closed
strategies above; otherwise author a compatible guarded source. Never run a
current indexed body through an older same-named definition. Complex components
whose renamed inputs cannot be normalized without rewriting embedded semantics
also require current context. Uniqueness remains subject to the separate current
hard-constraint rules below; no historical uniqueness is reintroduced after a
relaxation merely to make a lookup compile.

A generated visible view retains the current forward-migration declaration set
for shared output columns. It does not resurrect a retired predecessor transform
whose outputs already remain current columns in the compiled union. Historical
source and receipts remain intact. This preserves current native values and
migration custody instead of reinterpreting them with a retired input domain.
Transforms needed for fields removed from current source remain subject to the
ordinary truthful-projection and complete-adoption checks.

A generated visible view omits a predecessor-only historical down declaration
when all its outputs remain current columns whose generated domains admit the
current native values. Its immutable original declaration and receipt evidence
remain historical; the view does not reenter that retired projection or restore
stale preimages over native values. Downs needed for removed fields remain.
Exact original-source selection retains its projection/conflict behavior. Writer
adoption still enforces full domains, lifetimes and zero-unacknowledged hiding.

Shared tables retain current caps, immutability, final-existence constraints
and lifecycle guards. Generated source carries authored current constraints,
unique keys, expiry and opening declarations; the compiled view must preserve
current hard constraints, reference-deletion/lifetime shape and sync audience
or preparation refuses. Text constraints follow the explicit domain join, not
a separate implicit relaxation. Schema quotas cannot become weaker merely
because their current rule IR is unchanged. Unsupported structural transport
requires an authored compatible workflow; no arbitrary population choice or
new business default is generated.

Shared tables retain the **current authored authority**, including transition
bindings, read rules, payload guards and field ownership. The compiled bound IR
must equal current authority after declared identity alignment. A release switch
is a view over the same current authoritative rows, never restoration of a
historical database snapshot: deleted rows stay deleted and current values
stay current except declared truthful migrations. No resurrection or replay of
historical preimages over authored current images is authorized. With no
hiding and no domain enlarging beyond current, those row populations remain
the current populations; changing read visibility still follows exact current
authority.
For intentional hiding, IR equality alone is insufficient: §20.2 additionally
checks population-sensitive authority/guards under the actual writer. Removed old immutability/uniqueness
constraints follow explicitly relaxed current constraints. Newer-only tables
retain current rules without promising old clients expose new concepts. Native
or worker dependencies without a qualified authored bundle refuse. Undefined
fields, changed helper closures and incompatible workflows refuse rather than
weakening guards. Nullable result fields and carried/reused guards appear in
an action-focused checklist; successful mechanical adaptations are facts, not
11–42 mandatory decisions. The commissioning author's chosen visible-rollback
contract explicitly permits nullable/wider result domains; these are disclosed
as RESULT compatibility changes, never claimed to retain a nonnull v1 result
contract. Requiring nonnull results would contradict that requested end game.
Names and accepted input contracts remain preserved. Dormant restoration advice
names --restore-dormant.

Removing a defaulted predecessor column requires an authored truthful down.
The initial grammar has no acknowledged-absence escape for removed defaults.
Preview may suggest a cross-table expression from declared references/columns,
but it is advisory source for the author to review, never an inferred business
mapping. A false default is not a reversible representation.

Prepared source is immutable. Each parent manifest maps original target id to
numeric view id, executable digest, changed columns, carried guards and hiding
reason. The parent's full control digest includes the manifest; the view's full
control digest includes its metadata. Prepared views are usable only after the
parent's CAS activation commits and never count as ordinary latest or equal
forward source. Failed parents remove stages/views. Unknown admitted outcomes
retain them for history-authoritative recovery; uncommitted parents cannot
authorize selection. Before either equal-source no-op or retained-source reactivation, prepare the
complete desired view set privately and compare target/executable digest/hiding
reasons with that source's own committed manifest, verifying its entries. A
matching set permits no-op or identity reuse. A missing, stale or newly needed
mapping creates an ordinary registration parent using the same source and full
adoption/CAS validation; no view is selectable before that commit. Output and
JSON identify the equivalent retained source as sourceRelease. Source and old
manifests remain immutable. Temporary desired views are removed on no-op/reuse,
refusal and preview. This also upgrades unchanged legacy deployments without
needed registrations. Later equal source is a no-op once its desired set matches.
Retained-source reactivation and rollback preserve their own registry context.

Resolve the manifest belonging to the active ordinary release, or the
registered parent of the active compiled view, under the command lock. Never
borrow a later deployment's view across source branches: it may carry weaker
guards than the active source. If that context has no mapping, default selection
uses original source with the same writer visibility checks. Bind actual source
digest, expected release and activation revision to the existing CAS. ABA/equal-content switches change revision. Cold, hot,
preview and restart use that actual immutable source. Selection restores dormant
names implicitly, with full data/constraint/lifetime validation. Output and
releases identify original and actual source. --exact selects the original and
reports dormant hiding; it bypasses only default visible-view selection, never
applicable security/strict refusals, authority checks or migration validation.

#### Current independent intent and authored execution closure — Integration 23

A generated view must not restore a predecessor workflow that violates current
independent intent. Copying invariant declarations, table rules or `require`
statements alone is insufficient: a changed literal, helper, grant mutation,
write key, default, control path, returned value or system/time dependency can
change the reachable effect without changing any copied guard. This applies
both to newly declared intent and intent already present in the predecessor.
Finite challenge campaigns are regression evidence, not an equivalence proof.

The presence of an invariant never makes ordinary schema evolution require a
view. The wave-25 replay exposed the Integration 23 regression in the earlier
"any invariant plus changed code" rule: it falsely refused ten of fifty v2
deploys, including simple renames and a split with a typed down. View registration
uses structural widening and old-row foresight facts, including hiding and
misrepresentation, rather than a general executable-digest change.

Final-existence obligations compare compiled constraints under injective declared
table and column identities, including typed reference keys and ordered plain-index
columns. Index labels and source spelling are not semantic changes; missing or
